      "Upon termination of employment, settlement of the exchange or the purchased extra free days takes place in accordance with Article 57 paragraphs 3, 4, 5, and 7.",
      "In derogation from the foregoing in paragraph 7, for the employee as referred to in Article 2 paragraph 3 sub a CAO, the following applies: when an exchange involves a conversion of time into money or vice versa, the exchange rate is: ((12/260)/(the number of agreed working hours per week/5)) times 100. The percentage thus obtained then applies as the exchange rate of the monthly salary per hour (for a four-week salary, the number 12 in the said formula must be replaced by 13). No rights can be derived from the remuneration elements mentioned in this article if they are explicitly not applicable according to the CAO."
    ],
    [
      "Generation Pact (Article 74a)",
      "An employee of 62 years or older may request to work a certain percentage of the normal working hours according to Article 18 paragraph 1 CAO less, against a certain percentage of the original salary and full pension accrual based on the original salary. The employer has the choice whether or not to grant this request.",
      "The following options apply: - 60% of original working hours - 80% of original salary - 100% pension accrual or - 70% of original working hours - 85% of original salary - 100% pension accrual or - 80% of original working hours - 90% of original salary - 100% pension accrual.",
      "Bijlage 10C, the Reglement Generatiepact, is part of this CAO."
    ],
    [

      "For shop personnel employed by an employer as referred to in Article 77 paragraph 7 CAO, the following applies. The normal working time per day is a maximum of 9 hours (including the weekly late-night shopping).",
      "Auxiliary staff and on-call staff will be appointed or called up for at least two consecutive hours.",
      "Employees can be obliged to work for a maximum of 3 evenings per week, with the understanding that employees who were employed before June 1, 1996, must give their consent.",
      "The employee is entitled to a five-day work week and cannot be obliged to work on Sunday.",
      "Allowance for work during special hours (to be paid in money or free time): Monday to Friday 18:00-21:00: 33 1/3%; 21:00-07:00: 50%; Saturday 14:00-18:00: 33 1/3%; 18:00-24:00: 100%; Sunday and public holidays: 100%."
    ],
    [
      "Saturday Work for Employers (Article 88)",
      "The employer, as referred to in Article 77 paragraph 7, may, in consultation with the works council or the employee representation body or the employee, agree to include Saturday in the roster on the basis of voluntary participation of the individual employee. Employees who work on Saturday according to the roster will receive another day off during the week. For working on Saturday, an allowance of 50% per hour on top of the hourly wage applies. Working on Sunday should be considered an exception. Exceptions may be made for calamities and necessary service and repair.",
      "If a company decides to include Saturday in the roster, the normal working time for employees is between 07:00 and 18:00. For the employee, work on the following morning starts one hour later for each overtime hour worked by the employee after 23:00 on the previous day. The maximum working time, including overtime, averages 10 hours per day and 45 hours per week calculated over a period of 13 weeks. In addition, once a year, an average of 50 hours can be worked for a period of 4 weeks. The daily uninterrupted rest period is at least 11 hours."

      "Employment with AOW-eligible employees or pension recipients (Article 15)",
      "With an employee who has reached the AOW-eligible age or with an employee who receives a pension payout from the Pensioenfonds Metaal en Techniek, an employment contract can be entered into for a fixed term or for an indefinite period.",
      "If a fixed-term employment contract has been continued for a fixed term, no prior notice is required for its termination. Nor is prior notice required if two or more fixed-term employment contracts have succeeded each other with interruptions.",
      "If the employment contract has been entered into for an indefinite period, or if a fixed-term employment contract is continued without objection after the expiry of this period, the employment contract can be terminated by notice.",
      "Otherwise, the provisions of this CAO apply to the employment contract, unless otherwise agreed in writing.",
      "It is recommended to enter into employment contracts for (each time) a specific, short period of at least three months and at most one year.",
      "If notice is required, the total duration of the employment contract must be observed for determining the notice period (see Article 16 CAO and 7:672 BW), and furthermore, the consent of the UWV Werkbedrijf is required.",
      "Since Article 33 of the CAO applies to employees below the age of 65, the remuneration of an employee who has exceeded this age must be explicitly agreed upon. The intention is that the employee does not suffer a net income reduction for equal work.",

      "For employees aged 65 and older, different rules may apply regarding social security and/or pension than for employees younger than 65."

      "If an employee uses his saved balance under the Levensloopregeling, the following applies: 1. The leave can be taken both full-time and part-time; 2. The employee must submit a request for leave of less than 3 months in writing to the employer at least 3 months before the intended start date. A request for leave of 3 months or longer must be submitted in writing at least 6 months before that date; 3. The employer makes a decision on the request after consultation with the employee within one month of receiving the request; 4. The employer grants the request if the employee wishes to take leave for a period of no longer than 2 years immediately preceding the moment he retires."

    [
      "Forced Absence (Article 64)",
      "In case of forced absence as referred to in Article 7:628 of the Civil Code, the continued payment of the consequently due salary, increased by any applicable shift allowance, is limited to five days, unless the absence is due to the employer's fault. If two or more periods of forced absence follow each other with an interruption of no more than 3 days, during which the employee has performed paid work at the employer's instruction, these periods of forced absence are considered one period of absence for the application of the 5 days referred to in the previous sentence.",
      "In derogation from paragraph 1, the employer is not obliged to pay any salary, increased by any applicable shift allowance, if work cannot be performed due to forced absence caused by frost, strong wind, high water level, flood(s), ice, or snow/slipperiness/thaw at the workplace, subject to the provisions of paragraph 3 of this article.",
      "For every (full) day that work cannot be performed due to the causes referred to in the previous paragraph, the employer provides a supplement to the benefit granted to the employee under the Werkloosheidswet (Unemployment Act) up to 100% of the employee's applicable individually agreed salary, increased by any applicable shift allowance. For each period of frost or high water level, the aforementioned supplement must be paid for a maximum of two weeks. In a frost period or high water level period that has once begun, the two-week period is deemed to be interrupted if work has been performed for at least three consecutive days. After these three days, a new period of a maximum of two weeks of supplementation for the employer begins, if and insofar as there is still forced absence due to frost or high water level. If the employee does not receive a benefit under the Werkloosheidswet due to the so-called reference requirement, the employee retains the right to a salary supplement from the employer up to 100%.",
      "Upon the employer's introduction of a temporary reduction in working hours (including reduction to a 0-hour week) approved by the competent authority, the employer pays no salary for the time during which no work is performed.",
      "If the employee is entitled to a benefit under the Werkloosheidswet from the Uitvoeringsinstituut Werknemersverzekeringen (UWV) with which the employer is affiliated, because the employer is not obliged to pay salary, increased by any applicable shift allowance, under the provisions of the preceding paragraph, this benefit is supplemented by the employer up to the lost salary.",
      "If a person works less than 15 hours per week and the times at which work must be performed are not fixed, or if the scope of work is not or not unambiguously fixed, Article 7:628 BW does not apply for the first 12 months of the agreement, in derogation from paragraphs 1 to 5."

      "Definition of Incapacity for Work (Article 65)",
      "Incapacity for work in this chapter is understood to mean being unfit for work due to illness, accident, defect, pregnancy, and/or childbirth, all as described in the Ziektewet (ZW) and the Wet op de Arbeidsongeschiktheidsverzekering (WAO).",
      "The first day of incapacity for work is considered to be the day on which work was not performed or work was stopped during working hours.",
      "A day falling within the roster on which no work is performed due to reduced working hours is also considered a day of incapacity for work.",
      "Furthermore, a day on which no work is performed pursuant to a roster as referred to in Article 18 paragraph 4 is also considered a day of incapacity for work."
    ],

      "Reporting Incapacity for Work (Article 66)",
      "In case of incapacity for work, the employee is obliged to notify or have notified the employer as soon as possible. If the employee does not appear at work on the first day of incapacity, the notification referred to in the previous sentence must take place no later than 09:00 AM.",
      "To prevent abuse, the employer is authorized, if he makes it plausible that the employee has reported incapacitated for work incorrectly, either to consider the first day of incapacity for work as a leave day taken by the employee, or to withhold salary payment for that day."

      "Salary Supplementation (Article 67)",
      "a. The employer is obliged, in case of full or partial incapacity for work of the employee, to continue paying the employee the salary that the employee would have earned if fit for work, for a maximum period of 24 months, whereby 100% of that salary is paid for the first six months and 90% of that salary is paid for the following 18 months. b. In derogation from the provisions of paragraph 1 sub a, an employee who partially resumes work or resumes work on a work-therapeutic basis receives 100% of the salary that the employee would have earned if fit for work during the period of work resumption, for the maximum period of 24 months referred to in paragraph 1 sub a. c. In derogation from the provisions of paragraph 1 sub a, an employee who has been determined to have no chance of recovery and no remaining earning capacity receives 100% of the salary that the employee would have earned if fit for work during the maximum period of 24 months referred to in paragraph 1 sub a. If employer and employee doubt the applicability of the previous sentence, the employee may submit an application for an IVA benefit to the implementing body earlier than after two years. This application must be accompanied by a statement from the company doctor that, in his opinion, there is full and permanent incapacity for work and no chance of recovery. The company doctor's statement must also be based on a statement from the treating medical specialist.",

      "Notice of Termination (Article 16)",
      "Termination of an employment contract takes place with due observance of the terms as mentioned in Article 7:672 BW.",
      "Termination takes place with due observance of the notice periods against the end of the month for monthly salary payment and against the end of the four-week period for four-weekly salary payment.",
      "Article 7:672 BW: 1. Termination takes place against the end of the month, unless a different day has been designated by written agreement or by custom. 2. The notice period to be observed by the employer is, for an employment contract that on the day of termination: a. has lasted less than five years: one month; b. has lasted five years or longer, but less than ten years: two months; c. has lasted ten years or longer, but less than fifteen years: three months; d. has lasted fifteen years or longer: four months. 3. In derogation from paragraph 2, the notice period to be observed by the employer is one month if the employee has reached the age referred to in Article 7, part a, of the Algemene Ouderdomswet. 4. The notice period to be observed by the employee is one month. 5. If the consent, referred to in Article 671a, paragraph 1, has been granted, the notice period to be observed by the employer is shortened by the duration of the period that commences on the date on which the complete request for consent was received and ends on the date of the decision on the request for consent, with the understanding that a minimum period of one month remains. 6. The period, referred to in paragraph 2 or paragraph 3, can only be shortened by collective labor agreement or by regulation by or on behalf of a competent administrative body. The period can be extended in writing. 7. The period, referred to in paragraph 4, can be deviated from in writing. The notice period for the employee may not be longer than six months when extended, and for the employer not shorter than double that for the employee. 8. By collective labor agreement or by regulation by or on behalf of a competent administrative body, the notice period, referred to in paragraph 7, second sentence, for the employer may be shortened, provided the period is not shorter than that for the employee. 9. For the application of paragraph 2, employment contracts are deemed to form a single, uninterrupted employment contract in case of reinstatement of the employment contract pursuant to Article 682 or Article 683. 10. The party giving notice against an earlier day than applies between the parties owes the other party compensation equal to the amount of the salary fixed in money for the period that the employment contract should have continued with regular notice. 11.     [
      "Definitions (Article 17)",
      "Daily working time is understood to mean the time during which the employee performs work according to his roster.",
      "The day window is a period with a duration of 12 hours and runs from 06:00 to 18:00. If the daily working time falls within the day window, the allowance scheme according to Article 42a of this CAO does not apply. If the daily working time falls entirely or partially outside the day window, the allowance scheme according to Article 42a applies. The employer may shift the start time of the day window from 06:00 to 07:00 once a year for a period of one year, with the understanding that the day window also has a duration of 12 hours in this situation. The employer may, with the consent of the employee participation body or the personnel if no employee participation body is present, shift the start time of the day window to no later than 08:00; in this situation too, the end time of the day window is 12 hours later.",
      "Roster is understood to mean the schedule in which the daily working time applicable to the employee and the ADV-time are laid down.",
      "In derogation from the provisions of 3a, for 'flexible working time' (see Article 18a paragraph 2 sub b), the ADV-time is not laid down in the roster.",
      "ADV-time is understood to mean: the time during which no work is performed as a result of reduced working hours.",
      "Overtime is understood to mean: hours worked outside the roster. However, shifted hours as referred to in paragraph 6 are not considered overtime.",
      "Shifted hours are understood to mean: a. hours during which an employee works outside his roster, insofar as the employee, in thirteen consecutive weeks, including the said hours, does not work longer than the number of working hours according to his roster. The employer provides, at the employee's request, a periodic overview of the time and hours worked outside the roster, or the time and hours worked less than the number of hours according to his roster; b. hours during which, in consultation with the employee participation body or, in its absence, with the employee delegation, work is performed outside the roster, with the predetermined aim of making up for specifically designated hours during which no work is performed or will not be performed; c. hours during which an employee works outside his roster, but within the number of hours he would have worked based on his roster, due to the fact that the work cannot be performed within his roster due to circumstances at the client's company."
    ],
    [
      "Working Hours (Article 18)",
      "The normal weekly working time is, calculated over a period of a maximum of one year, an average of 38 hours, with due observance of the provisions of Article 17. For an employee who, based on an existing arrangement, works less than this average number of hours per week, the agreed number of hours applies.",
      "For the calculation of the average weekly working time, the hours that would have been worked according to the roster on New Year's Day, Easter Monday, Ascension Day, Whit Monday, both Christmas Days, the national holiday (April 27), and the days on which the employee is incapacitated for work, insofar as these days fall on a day within the roster, as well as the hours that would have been worked according to the roster on the short-leave days as referred to in Article 61, and on vacation days, also apply.",
      "The employer informs the employee of the applicable roster for a period of at least three weeks. In derogation from the foregoing, the roster may be changed in the interim, in consultation with the employee participation body or, in its absence, with the employee delegation, if this interim change is the result of a change in the form of reduced working hours.",
      "The employer may, in consultation with the employees' organizations (v.v.), and while maintaining the level of employment conditions applicable at the time of introduction, introduce a roster whereby work is performed on four days per week, which days can only be Monday to Friday, for a maximum of 9.5 hours per day.",
      "The employer determines the roster. If the employee requests it, the employer does so after consultation with the person concerned. The employer takes into account, insofar as can reasonably be expected of him, the employee's personal circumstances.",
      "In case of disagreement between the employer and the employee about the weighing of interests in determining the roster as referred to in paragraph 5, advice can be sought from the Vakraad.",
      "As a rule, no work is performed on Saturdays. If work must be performed regularly on Saturdays, the employer consults with the employee participation body or the employee delegation.",
      "An employee whose presence is regularly required on Saturdays will not perform work on another day, or two half days, per week.",
      "If an employee incidentally has to work on a Saturday, he may take a full day or two half days off at his own expense in the same or the following week, if desired."

      "Forms of Reduced Working Hours (Article 18a)",
      "The choice of the form of reduced working hours, as well as the change of choice, must be made in consultation with the employee participation body. In the absence of an employee participation body, the reduced working hours are established after consultation with the employee delegation. In addition, for companies listed by the employees' organizations (v.v.) with the Federatie Werkgeversorganisaties Metaaltechniek, there is also the possibility to change the choice in consultation with the v.v. If the employee starts working part-time, the employment contract must state whether ADV is taken in time or in money.",
      "One or more of the following options can be chosen, whereby ADV-time cannot be scheduled on Sundays and public holidays as referred to in Article 19 paragraph 1: a. ADV blocks - 8 consecutive ADV-hours per 4 weeks; - 4 consecutive ADV-hours per 2 weeks; - 2 consecutive ADV-hours per week. b. flexible working time - a week of at least 34 and at most 45 hours, whereby a minimum of 0 hours and a maximum of 9 hours can be worked per day. c. ADV days - This option is only available to an employer who performs work on construction sites. The (parts of) ADV days are, unless otherwise agreed in consultation with the employee participation body or the employee delegation, set on those (parts of) days on which the construction site is not accessible due to ADV. Any remaining (parts of) ADV days must be determined in consultation with the employee participation body or the employee delegation. d. free choice - In derogation from the provisions of paragraphs 1 and 2a to c, another form of reduced working hours can be chosen. This choice can only be made in consultation with the employee participation body or the employee delegation, or in consultation with the v.v. in the cases referred to in paragraph 1 of this article, penultimate sentence. If this is used, the chosen form of reduced working hours must be reported to the Vakraad."

      "Payment of Overtime (Article 42)",
      "The provisions of this article do not apply if the reimbursements concerned are included in the remuneration, which must be evident from a written statement issued by the employer.",
      "The employer pays the employee the following reimbursements: a. if work is performed outside the roster on a day that is not a Saturday, a Sunday, or a public holiday: - a reimbursement of 0.78% of the monthly salary (0.84% of the salary per four-week period) per hour for the first two overtime hours immediately preceding or immediately following the roster, whereby 'immediately preceding' or 'immediately following' also includes those overtime hours that are separated from the roster by a legally required rest period or a rest period required by local circumstances; - a reimbursement of 0.89% of the monthly salary (0.97% of the salary per four-week period) per hour for the overtime hours that follow the hours mentioned in the previous sentence; b. if work is performed outside the roster on a Saturday that is not a public holiday: a reimbursement of 0.89% of the monthly salary (0.97% of the salary per four-week period) per hour; c. if work is performed on a Sunday that is not a public holiday, a reimbursement of 1.12% of the monthly salary (1.21% of the salary per four-week period) per hour; d. if work is performed on a public holiday, a reimbursement of 1.12% of the monthly salary (1.21% of the salary per four-week period) per hour. This reimbursement amounts to 0.607% of the monthly salary (0.658% of the salary per four-week period) when time off is given on another day in the same or the following week, as a replacement for the hours to which that reimbursement relates.",
      "No reimbursement is due for overtime performed immediately following daily working hours, when this overtime serves to complete the normal daily task, occurs only incidentally, and does not last longer than half an hour. If this overtime is of longer duration, the reimbursement is due for its entire duration.",
      "If the overtime does not immediately follow the roster, but starts at a later time, and moreover, on the day concerned, the daily working time has been worked, the employer pays the employee a reimbursement of 0.89% of the monthly salary (0.97% of the salary per four-week period) per hour for all hours worked as part of the overtime. The said reimbursement also remains applicable if the hours referred to in the previous sentence, without an interruption of at least three consecutive hours, fall within the next daily working time.",
      "At the employee's choice, overtime and the allowances thereon are reimbursed in one of the following ways: 1. overtime and allowances are reimbursed in money; 2. overtime and allowances are converted for pension purchase; 3. overtime is reimbursed in paid free time, allowances in money or by payment for pension purchase.       "If the employee chooses the option mentioned under Article 42 paragraph 5a sub 3, the following provisions apply: - The paid free time rights obtained through overtime are taken in consultation between employer and employee. - If, at the end of the calendar year, the free time rights acquired through overtime have not been taken, they can, at the employee's request, be paid out, used for pension purchase, or carried over to the next calendar year.
